# S19 decisions (append-only)

- **2026-11-14, Frank: excellent code health before further development.** "My purpose now is to refactor the code
  such that code health becomes excellent and we can safely move on with the development to the future. This is the
  goal. Do what it takes." Consequence: a repo-wide campaign S19 runs before S18; the D19 interface package
  (`docs/S17_DECISIONS.md`, D19) is folded into it as ADR 0076. Evidence: repo-wide trial scan at d90b498f (debt
  6370.9, health 7.3, 44 brain functions, 10 import cycles; `docs/briefs/S19-digest.md`).
- **DECISION: the debt baseline `CB` = main d90b498f; the map base `MB` moves to milestone commits.** Alternative:
  one map at `CB` (or S17's 3fa71973). Rejected: `scripts/test_impact.py` falls back to the full suite for any added
  or deleted file, `docs/` included (`_fallback_rule`, B-16), and S19 adds design notes and split modules; every S17
  gate fell back (about 13.5 min each; at that rate S19 would need about 700 full-suite gates). So `MB` is rebuilt
  (about 52 min) after the design and config commits and after each batch of splits; `gate.sh` takes `MB` from
  `docs/S19_STATE.md`. The first build at d90b498f was stopped for this reason. Reversible by: setting `MB` = `CB`.
- **DECISION: design first (A1, `architect`) for the whole tree before any step.** Alternative: start function-level
  steps on the worst files at once. Rejected: 10 cycles across packages and seven files over 1000 NLOC need a target
  layer order and split plans first, or steps would be undone by the splits. Reversible by: none needed (no code).
- **2026-11-14, Frank: more agents allowed for S19.** "Do S19. You can use more agents if needed, I have decided to
  use a reset later this week." This relaxes the CLAUDE.md limit of 2 agents at a time for this campaign (the file is
  not edited). Applied as: up to 3 or 4 agents when their work is on disjoint files or read-only (engineers on
  separate branches per package group, a reviewer, the design lane); never two engineers on one branch. Missing
  codehealth features go to the code_health session (sent 2026-11-14: `arch --config`, cycle and package views
  without layers, per-package scan aggregation, the scan JSON schema, a branch-safe run log).
- **DECISION: re-pin cce-code-health a3401585 -> d542005 before the first step.** It adds `arch --config/--depth/--edges`,
  `scan --group-depth`, the scan JSON schema (`meta.schema = 1`) and a locked, branch-tagged run log (the S19 feature
  requests; gate tiers and comparators unchanged per the code_health session). Check at d90b498f: trial arch 10
  cycles, largest 17 nodes, 79 cycle edges; trial scan debt 6370.9 (as before). Parallel engineers run as worktrees
  of this clone (one run log). Alternative: keep a3401585 and hand-made graphs. Reversible by: the pin line.
- **2026-11-14, A1 judged (design note 5644d339, ADR 0076 proposed 9016d957).** Accepted: the repo-layers order and
  the forbid rule (config commit; adoption measured with the committed config: 14 violations, 10 cycles at depth 4,
  79 cycle edges, debt 6370.9), the cycle cuts A1 to A18, splits before function steps for files of 1000 NLOC or
  more, the sensitivity tiers and review cadence (S1 every 3, S2 and S1/S2 splits every 4, others every 8), and the
  end gate (section 6; health >= 9.0 binds).
- **DECISION Q1: the 35 F0 M9 modules stay frozen during W1 to W4; they get a phase of their own (W5) under one
  identity ruling.** Fact: `scripts/codehealth_frozen.py` lists 35 modules (models c1, syn001 and thermal units,
  thermo providers, `_coolprop_heos_fluids.py`) whose bytes feed `implementation_sha256` (F0 M9); together debt
  565.2, and 9 of them below health 7.0 (column_unit 4.9, pr_c1 4.6, iapws95 6.2, coolprop_heos 6.4,
  conversion_reactor 6.6, kinetic_cstr 6.8, mixer 6.9, pr_lib 6.9, wilson_ig 6.9). Any byte change re-registers
  model identity, a design-lane matter (replay identity). W5: a `specifier` ruling on re-registering M9 once for a
  batch of behaviour-identical edits (M1 to M8 unchanged), then the edits. Split B7 (`_coolprop_heos_fluids.py`, data
  generated by `docs/derivations/scripts/s07_reference.py --emit-fluid-table`; Q8 answered: generated) goes through
  the generator in W5. Alternative: re-register per step. Rejected: one identity change per batch is reviewable,
  one per step is not. Until W5 the end gate is measured honestly with these files in it.
- **DECISION: no test map until W2 ends; W1 and W2 gates run the full suite.** Almost every W1/W2 step adds a module
  (a leaf or a split), and `select` falls back on any added file. `MB` stays at `CB`, which has no map (the tests
  tier then falls back to the full suite by `FallbackError`). The map is built after W2 for W3. Alternative: a map now.
  Rejected: it would serve no W1/W2 gate.
- **DECISION Q2, Q4, Q5, Q6, Q7, Q10, Q11, FQ1 to FQ4: the design note's defaults.** Q9 (public-API tier on ADR steps
  that remove names): asked of the code_health session (an allow-list mode); default as the note says. Q3 (A15,
  R-016), A11 and I-10: design-lane rulings before W4. ADR 0076 gets a `reviewer` pass and goes to Frank before W4.
- **DECISION: W1 runs as two engineers in parallel, lane a (A1 to A4) in worktree `s19a` on `wp/S19-a`, lane b (A6
  to A8) in `s19b` on `wp/S19-b`, both from the config commit; the orchestrator merges them into `wp/S19` (merge
  commits). Each engineer writes its restructure plan as its own commit before the step (the gate reads the plan at
  the base); the orchestrator approves plans after the fact under D18's delegation, and a plan whose numbers miss
  stops the lane.** Alternative: one lane. Rejected: Frank allowed more agents; the lanes touch disjoint files.
- **2026-11-14, Frank (his own decision form): four decisions.** (1) Docs health enabled, warnings only: a `[docs]`
  table in `.codehealth/config.toml` with records (ADRs, decision logs, design history, reviews, briefs, handoffs,
  rc records, progress, decision register, evidence) kept as written; at this commit 762 docs, 467 broken
  references in living docs (to fix in a docs pass), 808 in records; docstrings off. (2) FQ-A: no shims; F0 M9
  implementation hashes and the built-in registry hash change once, in W5, under one design-lane ruling.
  (3) FQ-B: the catalogue's registry hook is installed by the registry, `application.errors` and the jobs worker;
  fresh-interpreter tests prove it. (4) FQ-C: the K06 facade `transactions.Application` is deleted; its behaviour
  moves to the service that owns runs, tests ported; ADR 0076 supersedes ADR 0019's facade paragraph.
- **2026-11-14, ADR 0076 revised (31e287dd, still proposed).** All review findings held and were applied
  (`docs/briefs/S19-ADR0076-review.md`): I-10 moves `route_structure` only; I-14 hook installers; I-15 a solver-owned
  `AdmissibilityRefusal` and a K copy pinned bitwise; I-19 keeps the re-exports frozen files need until W5; I-1 name
  `numerics.solve_record`; I-2 `models.lifted_tolerance` (also `PRESSURE_TOLERANCE` from orchestrator/rank.py:38).
  Open: Q-F1 (a `retargeted` key) and Q-F5 (patch-following) asked of the code_health session; defaults in the ADR.
  Next: Frank's answers folded into the ADR text; ADR to Frank for acceptance before W4.
- **2026-11-14: cce-code-health 1bf41a5 has the interface mode** (plan `.codehealth/interface/<id>.toml`; exact
  public_api; callers check). Re-pin at the W4 boundary.
- **2026-11-14: code_health releases after d542005 (re-pin before W4, target 04f630e or later).** 04f630e: interface
  mode with Q-F1 (`retargeted` key) and Q-F5 (patch targets follow renames, moves and retargets), the callers rule
  rewritten after a second review (deletions listed in the gate JSON); ADR 0076's Q-F1/Q-F5 defaults become "through
  the gate". dc80d9d: `codehealth stats` (per-recipe statistics from run logs). 965de38: a step that pushes a file
  over a size threshold fails the step gate (check at re-pin: W2 splits only shrink files; a W4 move may grow a home
  module). 0f7fe83 `docs --stale` (top entries are the M07/V02 specs: classify them as records in the docs pass) and
  c4143f4 `docs --agent` (9 agent docs, 6930 words, 3 repeated paragraphs): informational, for the docs pass.
- **DECISION: W2 runs as lane c (A5, then splits B1 and B8-executor: orchestrator and numerics) and lane d (B6, B5 with
  B5a first, B8-certificate: studies.trust_region and verify), both from the merge of W1; then a models lane (B3, B2,
  B4).** Alternative: A5 alone before any split, as the design note orders it. Rejected: A5 and B6/B5 touch disjoint
  files, splits have arch delta 0 / 0, and each lane re-measures at its base; the clone-wide gate lock already
  serializes the gates, so a third lane would add queueing, not throughput. B7 (frozen) waits for W5. A `reviewer`
  pass on the W1 moves (brief `S19-R-W1.md`) runs in parallel (S1/S2 leaf moves: every 4). Reversible by: running A5
  alone first.
- **DECISION: re-pin cce-code-health to 6fbc82b (or later) at the end of W2, not now.** 6fbc82b adds `codehealth log
  --rerun-of STEP --reason` (a passing re-gate of a tree whose records are all gate-fail; the earlier record stays,
  `stats` counts it as a re-run). Lanes c and d are gating with d542005; changing the tool under running lanes would
  give one lane two tool versions. At the re-pin: backfill A6 part 1 (`--gate-json
  runs/s19-gates/20261114T111827-s19b-A6a-r2.json --rerun-of 1 --head e56f1189 --reason "wall-time budget test flaky
  under load"`), and check 965de38's file-size gate against the W2 results. Alternative: re-pin now. Rejected: as
  above. Reversible by: the pin line.
- **2026-11-14, Frank: ADR 0076 accepted as written** (Decision Desk card `ofs-adr0076-accept`, option A, signed

- **2026-11-14, R-W1 (`reviewer` on the W1 moves, brief `S19-R-W1.md`): merge as is; no blocker.** Checked beyond the
  gate: `__all__` unchanged in all 17 touched modules, every base name still bound at its old path, no persisted
  `__module__`, no import-time registrations moved, patch_noop 0 of 243 plus a grep for patches of names the moved code

  (1) A8 `assessment/sizing/__init__.py` keeps 35 incidental imports as `X as X` re-exports (the public-API check
  counts every `__init__` import as public). Removing them removes public names, so it is an ADR 0076 W4 step (with

  rank's default tolerance, now `models.lifted_tolerance.PRESSURE_TOLERANCE` (same object as before A4, bitwise

  (3) Comment and docstring fixes (`--no-gain` steps): `models/lifted_tolerance.py:24-25` (dated reason; in lane c's
  brief), `assessment/linearization.py:3-4` ("these three": two) and its lazy import at 38-42 (now unnecessary): W3
  group G4.
  (4) Process: the A4 config edit was authorised by the orchestrator's addendum to lane a (SendMessage, 2026-11-14:
  "In A4's plan commit you may also add openflowsheet.models.lifted_tolerance to that forbid rule's `to` list"); the
  brief file was not updated. From now on addenda are appended to the brief file as well.
- **DECISION: a step that needs a test, script or test-data retarget gets an orchestrator-authorised retarget commit
  placed BEFORE its plan commit.** Order: retarget commit (tests/, scripts/ or tests/data only; patch targets, file
  paths or allowlist entries follow the moved code; no assertion, expected value or test case changes; at that commit
  alone the affected tests may fail), then the plan commit, then the step commit, gated against the plan commit. The
  gate's scope then covers only the step, and its full suite at the step's head runs with the retargeted tests. First
  use: A5 (lane c's gate failed only on `test_t07_global_state.py::test_g20...`, whose allowlist named
  `orchestrator/trace.py` for `INTERRUPT_CHECK`; retarget 42756ce1, re-based plan ae26a1da, step c0221eed).
  Alternative: retarget after the step and re-gate. Rejected: the scope check fails on any out-of-scope path in
  base..HEAD. Reversible by: none needed (each retarget is its own commit).
- **DECISION: B8-executor is skipped** (lane c): `orchestrator/executor.py` has no section headers; the
  split-declaration helpers need the `Flowsheet` alias that stays, the EO-recovery group reaches `StepResult` and
  `_region` and is patched by tests, and the only closed group is about 30 NLOC (85 with helpers). Two tiny modules
  would be fragmenting (rejected in S19_RULES). executor.py (1057 NLOC) is left for W3 function-level work, which also
  shrinks it.
- **DECISION: B1 (region split) proceeds with a retarget commit as above, not a new partition.** Lane c found the
  partition clean (closures: `region_phase` reaches nothing that stays; `region_problem` reaches only `region_phase`;
  `region_attempts` out), but test patches of `region.X` would stop reaching moved call sites: fully lost `_rederive`,
  `_reset_outlet`, `ph_state`; partly lost `check_opening`, `_contract_kernel`, `_pr_c1`, `_ph_closure`, `_kernel`,
  `_admissible` (tests t03, t05b, t08, opt_revision_host) and the same in `scripts/t03_evidence_manifest.py`,
  `scripts/t05b_evidence_manifest.py`. Any split of region meets the same patches, so a different partition does not
  help. The retarget patches every module that holds a call site of the patched name (both, where calls stay in
  region too). A `reviewer` reads the retarget diff before the lane is merged.
- **2026-11-14, lane d: B8-certificate accepted** (plan 7af19207, step ad7ad338 `verify/verdict.py`, gate 16/16; docs
  follow-up 97ef27ea 15/15). B6 and B5 stopped as the rules require; rulings:
- **DECISION B6: a third module `studies/trust_region/projection_support.py` holds what both new modules reach:
  `OMITTED_ROW_REASON`, `RefusalCode`, `ProjectionRefusedError`, `OmittedRow`, `_RowDerivatives`, `_float_rows`;
  `_Assembly`, `ZeroBlock`, `ZeroEliminated` move with `projection_zero_flows`.** projection.py re-exports every public
  name, so callers see the same objects at the same paths; the module for the result vocabulary is a W4 naming
  question, not a W2 one. Alternative: leave projection.py whole for W3. Rejected: 1343 NLOC does not fit a function
  step's review. Expected arch 0 / 0 at the base.
- **DECISION B5: the design note's B5a numbers (table 4.1: -1 cycle, -4 edges) were wrong.** `verify/gamma_phi_units.py:65`
  imports `MODEL_CHECKS`, `REACTING_MODELS`, two notes, `Degeneracy` and the flowsheet envelope rows
  (`_envelope_material`, `_envelope_energy`, table.py:1152-1257) from table at module level, while table imports
  gamma_phi_units lazily (table.py:1728); lane d's simulation of B5a gives 11/6/18 with that cycle intact. Ruling: B5
  is planned at its base by simulation (the plan's numbers are what `codehealth arch` gives on a scratch tree of the
  intended moves, written before the run). The goal is that no module-level import of gamma_phi_units or thermal
  reaches table: everything they import from table moves down with the B5 groups (unit_checks with
  `_ModelChecksTable` and `MODEL_CHECKS`, split_checks with `Degeneracy`), plus a fourth module for the flowsheet
  envelope rows if they are needed below table. `MODEL_CHECKS` stays one object (re-exported). STOP if a group's
  closure reaches what stays, or if any module mutates `MODEL_CHECKS` at import time in an order the move would change.
  Table 4.1's later rows are re-derived at each base anyway.
- **DECISION B1: accepted with expected arch 9 / 6 / 20 (one more violation) and region.py at 1270 NLOC.** Lane c
  part 2 simulated the split (scratch: ast.dump identical, mypy clean, 360 region tests pass, patch_noop 0). The moved
  admissibility functions carry their lazy `verify.checks` imports (region.py:617, 648, 673, 710), so the existing
  edge region -> verify.checks gains a twin region_phase -> verify.checks: the same dependency (R-016, A15), counted
  per module. No partition avoids it (keeping `_admissible` in region makes region_problem import region). A15 removes
  both edges. region.py stays a `large_file` (2056 -> 1270 NLOC) until W3's function steps on `_solve_region` and a
  later `region_attempts` move whose closure then allows it. Retarget set at this base: 22 patches (tests t03, t04,
  t05b, t08, opt_revision_host; scripts t03, t04, t05b evidence manifests); region keeps `ph_state as ph_state`.
  Alternative: no split until W3. Rejected: 2056 NLOC does not fit a function step's review.
- **DECISION B1 (revised): parked until ADR 0076 I-15 lands; I-15 is pulled forward to the first step after the W2
  re-pin.** B1's gate (20261114T153247-b1-region, run-log step 20) failed on `architecture`: the restructure ratchet
  (cce_code_health/architecture.py, `ratchet`) excuses a violation from a new file only when the same edge leaves the
  old module, and region.py keeps a lazy `verify.checks` import (`VerifierError`, region.py:401 at the step head), so
  `region_phase -> verify.checks` is new. The earlier "accepted at 9/6/20" entry is withdrawn: it would need the gate
  weakened. I-15 (accepted ADR; the solver stops importing the verifier, `AdmissibilityRefusal` in `models.lifted`)
  removes those imports, after which B1 is 0/0 and passes the ratchet. Alternatives: (a) a tool exception for a
  declared twin edge (rejected: a gate exception for a dependency the ADR removes anyway); (b) reroute region's own
  `VerifierError` import through region_phase (rejected: a non-verbatim change to an S1 file to satisfy a counter).
  The retarget (99dfc975), plan (3799bcd4) and step (4f036b4b) are kept on `wip/S19-B1`; after I-15 they are rebuilt at
  the new base. Its docstring follow-up must also fix `studies/nlp/revision_host.py:93, 622` (they name region.py as
  the home of `_region_problem` and `admissibility_value`). wp/S19-c (A5 and follow-ups) merged as 537e32a7: arch 8 /
  6 / 20, patch_noop 0 of 243.

- **2026-11-14, I-15 (lane f, wp/S19-f): rulings on the engineer's stop.** Tests-first commit a68186eb (18 forced-refusal
  tests through the provider, passing at the base; `test_s10c_region.py:109` relaxed to `RuntimeError`, re-pinned
  after the step). (1) **DECISION: `scripts/m07_journey.py:466` gets identical handling in a script commit right
  after the step**; ADR 0076 I-15 carries a factual addendum (the handler list missed it; the rule "every handler that
  catches a route solve's `VerifierError`" already covers it). Alternative: before the step (rejected: the name does
  not exist yet; catching `RuntimeError` would widen it). Between the two commits a converged-path refusal would
  escape the journey script; no run reaches that path today (Q-F6). (2) **DECISION: the K pin reads the `(T, P)`
  states of the F0 corpus bundles if they hold the lifted splits' states; otherwise a spy during the F0 solves, marked
  slow.** (3) TR parent :464 gets a forced test if one can reach it through the provider; otherwise the gap is named

- **DECISION (W3 cadence): a W3 run that touches tier S1 files stops after 4 accepted S1 steps, and every W3 run gets a
  `reviewer` read before its branch merges.** The design note's cadence (S1 every 3, S2 every 4, others every 8) is
  kept in spirit: an engineer run is the review unit, and S1 runs are capped at 4 steps so a systematic error is
  caught within one batch. Alternative: a reviewer every 3 S1 steps mid-run (rejected: the engineer would stop and
  wait, and a resumed engineer rereads its whole context). Targets per group: `<git-common-dir>/codehealth/briefs/S19-W3-targets.md`
  (scan at df29751f: debt 6370.9, health 7.69, 44 brain functions); the common brief `S19-W3-common.md`. First pair:

- **DECISION 2026-11-16, STOP 1 of W4 run 2 (re-exports outside `__init__.py`).** cce-code-health counts imports as
  public API only in `__init__.py` (gate.py:289), so an interface plan cannot list `removed` for a re-export in
  region.py, verification.py, program.py or types.py. Ruling: ADR 0076 rule 1's own test is "no alias, no
  `__getattr__`, no `__all__` entry". A step that drops such re-exports lists `changed = ["<file>:__all__"]` where
  the module has `__all__` (region.py, program.py), and nothing for an alias in a module without one (types.py,
  verification.py); a module may keep a plain import of a name its own code uses. The plan's reason names every
  dropped alias, the step's message quotes the grep that finds no importer of the old path, and the reviewer checks
  both. I-5's interface step (6f85454c) is gated no-gain for that reason (job 31). Alternative: tool change (c), an
  `X as X` import counts as API in any module, with a re-pin; rejected for now because it changes every lane's
  public-API tier mid-campaign. Reversible by: re-gating those steps after a tool change.
